Core
Brave AI
Q
  • Top 50 Books On Feminism In Hindi Are
A
Intro
  • A complete list of the top 50 books on feminism in Hindi is not available in the provided search context, which only lists a limited collection of Hindi books on the topic at Pustak.org and highlights key works in other languages.
  • While a specific top 50 Hindi list is not provided, the following works are frequently cited as essential reading for understanding feminism in the Indian context, including several originally written in Hindi:
Hindi and Indian Feminist Literature Recommendations
  • Tomb of Sand by Geetanjali Shree: A powerful postmodern novel translated from Hindi that explores patriarchal structures in the Indian middle class.
  • Mai by Geetanjali Shree: A collection of profound short stories translated from Hindi, focusing on love, loss, and female experiences.
  • Tohellwithyou Mitro by Krishna Sobti: An unapologetic portrayal of a married woman who defies household boundaries and sexual constraints.
  • Pinjar by Amrita Pritam: A seminal novel set during the Partition of India, highlighting the struggles of women amidst communal violence.
  • That Long Silence by Shashi Deshpande: A significant work exploring the life of a woman negotiating her identity within marriage and society.
Other Essential Indian Feminist Works
  • For a broader understanding, the following non-Hindi but highly recommended Indian feminist books are frequently mentioned:
  • Seeing Like a Feminist by Nivedita Menon: An accessible introduction to intersectional feminism, covering topics like the Pink Chaddi campaign and personal laws.
  • Chup: Breaking the Silence About India’s Women by Deepa Narayan: Based on interviews with women across India, it examines societal expectations of "good" women.
  • Lifting the Veil by Ismat Chughtai: A blend of fiction and non-fiction by one of India’s earliest feminist writers, exploring female sexuality and boldness.
  • My Story by Kamala Das: An autobiography that fearlessly articulates taboo subjects and the desire for female love and transcendence.
  • Me Hijra, Me Laxmi by Laxmi Narayan Tripathi: A memoir detailing the life of a prominent hijra activist and their fight for gender identity and rights.

4.8.2.3.1. Brave AI
Q
  • Top 50 Books On Feminism In Hindi Are
A
Intro
  • A complete list of the top 50 books on feminism in Hindi is not available in the provided search context, which only lists a limited collection of Hindi books on the topic at Pustak.org and highlights key works in other languages.
  • While a specific top 50 Hindi list is not provided, the following works are frequently cited as essential reading for understanding feminism in the Indian context, including several originally written in Hindi:
Hindi and Indian Feminist Literature Recommendations
  • Tomb of Sand by Geetanjali Shree: A powerful postmodern novel translated from Hindi that explores patriarchal structures in the Indian middle class.
  • Mai by Geetanjali Shree: A collection of profound short stories translated from Hindi, focusing on love, loss, and female experiences.
  • Tohellwithyou Mitro by Krishna Sobti: An unapologetic portrayal of a married woman who defies household boundaries and sexual constraints.
  • Pinjar by Amrita Pritam: A seminal novel set during the Partition of India, highlighting the struggles of women amidst communal violence.
  • That Long Silence by Shashi Deshpande: A significant work exploring the life of a woman negotiating her identity within marriage and society.
Other Essential Indian Feminist Works
  • For a broader understanding, the following non-Hindi but highly recommended Indian feminist books are frequently mentioned:
  • Seeing Like a Feminist by Nivedita Menon: An accessible introduction to intersectional feminism, covering topics like the Pink Chaddi campaign and personal laws.
  • Chup: Breaking the Silence About India’s Women by Deepa Narayan: Based on interviews with women across India, it examines societal expectations of "good" women.
  • Lifting the Veil by Ismat Chughtai: A blend of fiction and non-fiction by one of India’s earliest feminist writers, exploring female sexuality and boldness.
  • My Story by Kamala Das: An autobiography that fearlessly articulates taboo subjects and the desire for female love and transcendence.
  • Me Hijra, Me Laxmi by Laxmi Narayan Tripathi: A memoir detailing the life of a prominent hijra activist and their fight for gender identity and rights.

Q
  • Top 20 Texts On Women In Journalism In India Are
A
Introduction
  • Based on the provided search context, there is no specific ranking or list titled "Top 20 texts" on women in journalism in India. The available information highlights key reports, studies, and notable journalists rather than a curated list of 20 specific publications.
Key Reports and Studies
  • Global Media Monitoring Project (GMMP) 2020 Report: Titled Who Makes The News, this report documented a sharp decline in female participation, noting women form only 13% of print reporters and 20% of radio newscasters in India, despite being 52% of TV news reporters.
  • UN Women Report (2021): Gender Inequality in Indian Media revealed that women occupied barely 5% of leadership positions in newsrooms from 2018 to 2019. It also noted that 85% of panelists on English debate shows were men.
  • RSF 2026 World Press Freedom Index: Specifically noted that women make up less than 15% of guests on major evening talk shows and highlighted the severe online harassment, including doxxing and death threats, faced by female journalists.
  • Academic Studies: Various papers such as Gender and Media: Status of Women Journalist in Hindi Print Media in India and The Fine Print: India's leading English dailies: A feminist study of the gendered practice of journalism analyze the systemic under-representation and objectification of women in the field.
Notable Female Journalists and Anchors
Introduction
  • The context identifies several prominent figures shaping the narrative:
Misc
  • Investigative & Political: Barkha Dutt (Mojo Story), Anjana Om Kashyap (Aaj Tak), Sweta Singh (Aaj Tak), and Nidhi Razdan (Former NDTV).
  • Business & Finance: Shereen Bhan (CNBC-TV18), Menaka Doshi (Bloomberg), and Sonia Shenoy (ET Now).
  • Sports & Independent: Mayanti Langer (Star Sports), Faye D’Souza (Independent), and Shaili Chopra (Independent).
  • Emerging Voices (2025 Headlines): Sagarika Kissu, Saumya Khandelwal, and Divya Arya were recognized for their work on marginalized communities and identity politics.
